Date: 2026-09-24 (resumed 2026-09-25) · Goal: every Classroom page and the Question screen fit and read well on any screen size, with no scrolling for the must-see parts.

## Why pages feel zoomed in
Chrome zoom is 100%. Windows display scaling is 125% (its default on 1080p laptops), so a
1920x1080 screen gives the page 1536x695 CSS px. Measured in his Chrome: devicePixelRatio 1.25,
innerWidth/innerHeight 1536x695. Most students on Windows laptops see the same.

## Names (written into the app glossary, src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md)
- **Home**: `/classroom`. "home", "homepage", "dashboard" all mean this.
- **Drill page**: `/classroom/drill`, the Drill item under Home. Always said with "page".
  A plain "drill" is a set of questions a student works through.
- **Question screen**: the split view where a question is answered (`/drill`). RC version is
  the RC question screen.
- The Activity Centre's old "Homepage" is now **Activity start page**.

## Decisions
1. Target 1536x695. Also check a big monitor (~1920x950) and a narrow window (rail folds to menu).
2. Drill page first screen, no scrolling: finder row, Smart Drill, Build the drill you need,
   Resume your drill, Top priority, first two Assigned work items. Accuracy trend may scroll.
3. Home first screen, sized for a student WITH homework: greeting with name on one line, what's
   due + Continue, Smart Drill / Timed section, Assigned work card, latest message, AND Next
   seven days.
4. Same layout and design, tightened. If that cannot meet 2 or 3, show Joshua a specific
   rearrangement before building it.
5. Tighten only on short screens; big monitors keep today's roomy look.
6. Build on a branch, send before/after screenshots at 1536x695 and a big monitor. Nothing ships
   to lsatprep.study until Joshua approves.

## Order
Drill page, then Home, then the Question screen (own grilling round: auto-fit text per question,
15px floor, LR columns scroll independently like RC), then the Classroom rail (low priority).

They were only for previewing Home, and the Question screen work needs a new branch off main anyway. Removing them means reverting one file and deleting one untracked file, so I need your yes first.

While I'm there, I also recommend restoring `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` with `git checkout --`. The Lovable plugin gutted it when the dev server started, and my last restore attempt was blocked. It's a revert back to what's committed, not new work.

**2. The Smart Drill session on the tester account.** I recommend leaving it. It's a test account, so a half-finished session there doesn't hurt anyone. It also gives me a ready question to measure against in this round. Say so if you'd rather I clear it.

## Question screen, round 9

Here's where it stands today at 1536×695. The page is 931px tall. Choice E ends at 768, which is 73px below the bottom of the screen. The whole LR page scrolls because the LR frame doesn't fill the screen. The RC question screen already fills it, and each of its columns scrolls on its own.

**Q9.1: When a question is too long to fit, what should give first?**

Here's what I recommend, in this order:
1. **Shrink the header, breadcrumb and toolbar.** Every question gets that space back, and nothing a student reads gets smaller.
2. **Size the text to each question**, but never below a 15px floor. A median question (1,041 characters) should then fit at 1536×695 with the text barely smaller than today.
3. **Only the longest questions scroll**, like PT114-S2-Q23 at 2,412 characters. Those scroll inside their own column, the way RC does. The page itself never scrolls, so the toolbar and the next/submit controls stay on screen.

The tradeoff: with step 2, text size changes a little from one question to the next. The other way is to keep one size for everything and let long questions scroll. The text stays steady, but more questions end up with a scrollbar.

Do you want text sized per question with the 15px floor, or one fixed size with more questions scrolling?

## user

What's the danger of each option because I don't want to mess anything up? I'm very worried about that.

## assistant

Neither option can touch student data. This is a display-only change: no database, no saved answers, no completed work. The worst case either way is something that looks wrong, and one revert fixes that. It also can't reach students without your approval. It gets built on its own branch, I check it at every screen size and send you screenshots, and nothing publishes until you say so.

So the real risks are about how the screen behaves.

## Risks of sizing text per question

- **It can fight a student's own text-size setting.** The Question screen already has a text-size control. If auto-fit overrides it, a student who chose larger text gets it shrunk. That's the most serious risk, and it can be avoided, but only if the build gets it right.
- **Flicker.** To fit the text, the page has to measure it after it appears. Students could see text jump from one size to another as a question loads, or when fonts load late.
- **It's less like the real test.** On LawHub the text stays one size. A student practising with sizes that shift from question to question is practising on something the test won't give them.
- **More code, more ways to break.** Measuring and resizing is the fiddly kind of code that breaks on screen sizes nobody tested.

## Risks of one fixed size with scrolling

- **A student could miss choice E.** This is the bug you reported in the first place. If the choices column has to scroll, E can sit just out of sight. The fix is a clear cue, like a fade and a "more below" hint, so nobody thinks there are only four choices.
- **Scrolling in two columns can confuse at first.** The mouse wheel scrolls whichever column the pointer is over. RC already works this way, so students have seen it, but it's still a small change for LR.
- **Long questions still need a scroll.** Only the long ones, though. Once the header is shrunk, most questions should fit.

## My recommendation has changed

I recommended per-question sizing before. With your worry in mind, I now recommend **one fixed size with scrolling**:
- Shrink the header, breadcrumb and toolbar.
- Keep the text at the size the student chose.
- Let each column scroll on its own, like RC and like LawHub, with a clear cue whenever a choice is out of sight.

It has fewer moving parts, it can't override a student's setting, and it matches the real test. Its one serious risk, a hidden choice E, has a simple fix I can check at every screen size.

Should I lock that in and move to the next question?

## assistant

Understood. The goal is that the whole question and all five choices are in view without scrolling. Scrolling becomes a rare fallback, not the plan.

I checked the question data to see how hard that is. These are character counts across all 3,965 LR questions:

| | Typical question | 90% of questions are shorter than | Longest |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stimulus (left column) | 388 | 547 | 868 |
| Stem + five choices (right column) | 630 | 955 | 2,050 |

**The stimulus isn't the problem. The choices are.** Every stimulus is short, and the left column is capped at a narrow reading width, so it has room to spare. The right column is where the page overflows. On the question I measured, the header, breadcrumb and toolbar take the top 248px before the question starts, which is over a third of a 695px screen.

## How we get there without shrinking anyone's text

1. **Shrink the header, breadcrumb and toolbar**, from 248px to roughly 100px. That gives about 150px back to every question.
2. **Give the choices column more width.** It's the one that runs long, and the stimulus doesn't need the extra width. A wider column means fewer lines, so the choices take less height.
3. **Tighten the gaps between choices** without making the click targets small.

None of this changes text size, overrides a student's text setting, or touches their data. It's the lowest-risk way to meet your goal.

My rough estimate is that these three steps put the typical question fully in view at 1536×695, and most of the longer ones too. That estimate comes from character counts, not measurement. So I'll build these three first, then measure every one of the 3,965 questions on the real screen at each laptop size. You'll get the actual number that fit and the list of ones that don't.

## The decision that's still yours

Some of the longest questions will probably still overflow. For those, we'd either:
- **shrink their text slightly**, never below 15px and only on those questions, or
- **let the choices column scroll**, with a clear "more below" cue.

I'd decide that once we know how many questions it affects. If it's 20, the answer is different than if it's 400.

One more thing: a student who has turned their text size way up has chosen bigger text over fitting. For them, I'd keep their size and let the column scroll. Their setting wins.

Should I lock in steps 1 to 3 and the measure-first approach, then move to the next question, which is how the header should compress?

  notesPanelTab,
  onNotesPanelTabChange,
  notesPanelExplanation,
  notesPanelSimilar,
  notepadEnabled = true,
  fillViewport,
}: Props) {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const rawName = (user?.user_metadata?.display_name as string | undefined)?.trim();
  const welcomeName = rawName && rawName.length > 0 ? rawName.split(/\s+/)[0] : "Joshua";

  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "bg-[#1A1635] flex flex-col",
        fillViewport ? "h-screen overflow-hidden" : "min-h-screen",
      )}
      style={{ fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif' }}
    >
      <header className="bg-white border-b border-slate-200">
        <div className="px-4 sm:px-6 pt-2 pb-1.5 flex flex-wrap items-center justify-end gap-2 text-[13px] text-slate-700">
          {tutorToggleVisible && onTutorModeChange && (
            <>
              {/* Desktop: switch + label */}
              <div className="hidden sm:flex items-center gap-1.5">
                <GraduationCap className="w-4 h-4 text-slate-600" />
                <label htmlFor="official-tutor-toggle" className="cursor-pointer select-none font-medium">
                  Tutor
                </label>
                <Switch
                  id="official-tutor-toggle"
                  checked={!!tutorMode}
                  onCheckedChange={onTutorModeChange}
                  className="scale-90 data-[state=checked]:bg-[#7f1d1d]"
                />
              </div>
              {/* Mobile: icon-only round toggle */}
              <button
                type="button"
                onClick={() => onTutorModeChange(!tutorMode)}
                aria-label={tutorMode ? "Turn Tutor Mode off" : "Turn Tutor Mode on"}
                aria-pressed={!!tutorMode}
                className={cn(
                  "sm:hidden h-7 w-7 rounded-full flex items-center justify-center border transition-colors",
                  tutorMode
                    ? "bg-[#7f1d1d] text-white border-[#7f1d1d]"
                    : "bg-white text-slate-600 border-slate-300 hover:bg-slate-100",
                )}
              >
                <GraduationCap className="w-4 h-4" />
              </button>
              <span aria-hidden className="w-px h-5 bg-slate-300" />
            </>
          )}
          <span className="font-medium">Welcome, {welcomeName}</span>
        </div>
        {header && (
          <div className="px-4 sm:px-6 pb-2 pt-0.5">
            {header}
          </div>
        )}
      </header>
      <main className="flex-1 px-2 sm:px-4 pt-3 sm:pt-4 pb-3 sm:pb-5 flex relative overflow-hidden">
        <div className="flex-1 flex min-w-0">
          <div
            className={cn(
              "mx-auto w-full max-w-[1400px] bg-white text-black rounded-md border border-slate-300 shadow-[0_4px_18px_rgba(0,0,0,0.35)] overflow-hidden flex flex-col transition-[filter,opacity]",
              fillViewport ? "h-full" : "min-h-[700px]",
              isPaused && "blur-[3px] opacity-60 pointer-events-none select-none",
            )}
            aria-hidden={isPaused ? "true" : undefined}
          >
            {children}
          </div>
        </div>

        {/* Docked Notes panel (Smart Drill). md+: pushes the card; mobile: overlay drawer. */}
        {notesPanelEnabled && (
          <>
            {/* md+ push column */}
            <div
              className={cn(
                "hidden md:block shrink-0 overflow-hidden transition-[width] duration-300 ease-out",
                notesPanelOpen ? "w-[380px] pl-2 sm:pl-4" : "w-0",
              )}
            >
              {notesPanelOpen && (
                <DrillNotesPanel
                  onClose={onNotesPanelClose}
                  meta={notesPanelMeta}
                  activeTab={notesPanelTab}
                  onTabChange={onNotesPanelTabChange}
                  explanation={notesPanelExplanation}
                  similar={notesPanelSimilar}
                />
              )}
            </div>

            {/* mobile scrim + overlay drawer */}
            {notesPanelOpen && (
              <button
                type="button"
                aria-label="Close notes"
                onClick={onNotesPanelClose}
                className="md:hidden absolute inset-0 z-30 bg-black/20"
              />
            )}
            <div
              className={cn(
                "md:hidden absolute inset-y-0 right-0 z-40 max-w-[100vw] transition-transform duration-300 ease-out",
                notesPanelOpen ? "translate-x-0" : "translate-x-full pointer-events-none",
              )}
            >
              <DrillNotesPanel
                onClose={onNotesPanelClose}
                meta={notesPanelMeta}
                activeTab={notesPanelTab}
                onTabChange={onNotesPanelTabChange}
                explanation={notesPanelExplanation}
                similar={notesPanelSimilar}
              />
            </div>
          </>
        )}

        {isPaused && (
          <div
            role="dialog"
            aria-modal="true"
            aria-label={`${pauseNoun} paused`}
            className="absolute inset-0 z-50 flex items-center justify-center bg-black/40 backdrop-blur-sm"
          >
            <div className="bg-white rounded-md shadow-2xl border border-slate-200 px-10 py-8 max-w-md w-[92%] text-center">
              <h2 className="text-[20px] font-semibold text-slate-900 mb-2">{pauseNoun} Paused</h2>
              <p className="text-[14px] text-slate-600 mb-6">
                The timer is paused. Click Resume to continue your {pauseNoun.toLowerCase()}.
              </p>
              <button
                type="button"
                onClick={onResume}
                className="inline-flex items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-md bg-[#7f1d1d] hover:bg-[#991b1b] text-white px-6 py-2.5 text-[14px] font-semibold min-w-[160px] transition-colors"
              >
                <Play className="w-4 h-4" />
                Resume
              </button>
            </div>
          </div>
        )}
      </main>
      {!isPaused && !notesPanelEnabled && notepadEnabled && <DrillNotepad />}
    </div>
  );
}

## assistant

Q9.1 is locked and written to the plan file. Then I measured the live Question screen at 1536×695 on the tester account and found a bigger problem than long questions.

## Every LR question needs a scroll just to reach Next

Here's what takes up the height on a short question, top to bottom:

| Band | Height | What's in it |
|---|---|---|
| Welcome row | 35px | "Welcome, tester" (plus the Tutor switch when it's shown) |
| Title row | 45px | PrepTest name, Section/Question, Answer, Explanation, ?, Pause |
| Dark gap | 16px | |
| Toolbar | 53px | Directions, Find, highlighters, text size, timer, notes |
| **Question** | **534px** | stimulus + stem + choices |
| Question-number strip | 51px | ends at 737, **already below the 695 fold** |
| End / Previous / Next | 72px | ends at 809, **completely off screen** |

The controls around the question take about 295px, over 40% of a 695px screen. Previous and Next sit entirely below the fold. A student has to scroll before they can move on, even when the question itself fits. I checked this on PT101-S2-Q1, which has short choices.

## Q9.2: How should the controls shrink?

My recommendation:
1. **Previous and Next are always on screen.** They get pinned to the bottom, and the question area takes whatever height is left. This matters more than anything else in this round.
2. **Merge the question-number strip and End / Previous / Next into one bottom bar**, about 50px instead of 123px.
3. **Fold "Welcome, tester" (and the Tutor switch) into the title row.** That removes a whole row and saves 35px. Nothing disappears, it just moves.
4. **Trim the toolbar and the dark gaps.** Same buttons, less padding.

That brings the controls down from about 295px to about 150px. At 1536×695, that leaves roughly 545px for the question, with Next always visible. Every button stays, and none of them change what they do. Only their positions and spacing change.

The risk is that students who are used to today's screen will see the bottom bar laid out differently. Nothing breaks or goes missing, and the pieces stay in the same order as LawHub's bottom bar.

Can I lock in all four, or is there a button you don't want moved?

## Q9.3: Which screens get the new layout?

The header and bottom bar come from one shared frame. Five screens use it:
1. The LR Question screen (Smart Drill, drills, PrepTest sections)
2. The RC question screen
3. Blind Review
4. The section review walk-through
5. Recall's section stage in the Activity Centre

**I recommend all five.** They already share the frame, so the change reaches all of them by default. Leaving some on the old look would mean writing extra code to keep two versions alive, which is more code and more risk, not less. RC already fills the screen, so it mostly just gains the extra height. I'll check every one of the five at each laptop size before anything goes to you.

The alternative is LR only for now, with the other four in a later round. That keeps this change smaller, but the four would miss out, including Next being hidden below the fold on RC too, if it has the same problem.

All five, or LR only? Once you answer, the grilling is done. I'll branch off main, build it, run the 3,965-question measurement, and send you before and after screenshots. Nothing publishes until you approve.

              skipDisabled={currentAttempted || !!timer?.isPaused}

          ) : isFullSection ? (
            // A real timed section is not a submit-one-at-a-time drill: the
            // answer commits the moment it is selected, so Skip and Submit have
            // nothing to do. What the section needs is the official navigator —
            // the oval strip for the whole queue and prev/next arrows in the
            // bottom-right corner, exactly where the paper-and-LawHub interface
            // puts them. Leaving the section runs through Complete Section in
            // the header strip.
            <OfficialAnswerNavigator
              questionQueue={session.questionQueue}
              currentIndex={session.currentIndex}
              attemptedQids={attemptedQids}
              jumpEnabled={jumpEnabled}
              jumpDisabledReason={timer?.isPaused ? 'Paused — resume to navigate' : undefined}
              showQuestionList
              prevDisabled={session.currentIndex <= 0 || !!timer?.isPaused}
              nextDisabled={
                officialNextDisabled ||
                session.currentIndex >= session.questionQueue.length - 1
              }
              nextDisabledReason={
                timer?.isPaused
                  ? 'Paused — resume to navigate'
                  : session.currentIndex >= session.questionQueue.length - 1
                  ? 'Last question — use Complete Section to finish'
                  : undefined
              }
              onJumpTo={handleJumpTo}
              onPrev={officialPrev}
              onNext={() => { void officialNext(); }}
              revealEnabled={revealSealed ? false : sectionRevealEnabled}
              onRevealEnabledChange={revealSealed ? undefined : setSectionRevealEnabled}
            />
          ) : (

            // queue the student hand-picked from the Smart LR Drill card. Submit
            // saves and reveals in place (honoring the auto-reveal toggle); Next
            // walks the queue via the shared officialNext; Skip advances past the
            // answer gate; End finishes the section.
            <OfficialSmartDrillBar
              autoReveal={autoReveal}
              // A fixed drill has no auto-reveal switch: Answer and Explanation
              // in the strip are the review controls. The preference is left
              // alone for Smart Drill.
              onAutoRevealChange={reviewPolicy === 'fixed' ? undefined : handleAutoRevealChange}
              primaryAction={currentAttempted || currentUnscored ? 'next' : 'submit'}
              primaryDisabled={!selectedAnswer || !!timer?.isPaused || actionInFlight}
              onSubmit={() => {
                void (async () => {
                  const submitted = selectedAnswer;
                  await handleSubmitNonAdaptive(submitted);
                  if (autoReveal) {
                    setShowAnswerRevealed(prev => new Map(prev).set(currentQuestion.qid, true));
                  }
                  // Pop the AI tutor (post-answer when revealed) on a wrong answer
                  // so the student gets a specific, back-and-forth explanation
                  // instead of the old dead "ANALYSIS VALID" placeholder.
                  const tutorLaunch = resolveTutorLaunch({
                    flagEnabled: AI_TUTOR_ENABLED,
                    tutorModeEnabled: tutorMode,
                    correct: submitted === currentQuestion.correctAnswer,
                    answerRevealed: autoReveal,
                  });
                  if (tutorLaunch.shouldOpen) {
                    setAnswerLocked(true);
                    setTutorQuestionSnapshot(currentQuestion);
                    setTutorUserAnswerSnapshot(submitted);
                    setTutorChatMode(tutorLaunch.mode);
                    setTutorChatOpen(true);
                  }
                })();
              }}
              onNext={() => { void officialNext(); }}
              // A built set is a fixed list, so it walks both ways. Skip only
              // ever went forward, and only before the question was answered,
              // which left no way back to a question already worked — the one
              // thing reviewing a set is for.

}

/**
 * Smart Drill (adaptive) bottom action bar. Replaces the arrow navigator used
 * by fixed-queue modes. Left: End and Menu. Right: auto-reveal toggle, the
 * queue walk (Previous/Next for a fixed queue, Skip for the adaptive one),
 * and a primary Submit/Next pill. Answer and Explanation deliberately are not
 * here — they sit in the header strip (`OfficialSessionStrip`), because four
 * controls crowded this bar's left corner.
 */
export default function OfficialSmartDrillBar({
  autoReveal,
  onAutoRevealChange,
  primaryAction,
  primaryDisabled,
  primaryLabel,
  primaryVisible = true,
  onSubmit,
  onNext,
  onSkip,
  skipDisabled,
  onPrev,
  prevDisabled,
  onAdvance,
  advanceDisabled,
  pager,
  endLabel,
  onEnd,
  onOpenMenu,
  disabled,
}: Props) {
  const isNext = primaryAction === "next";

  return (
    <div className="border-t-2 border-slate-300 bg-white shadow-[0_-2px_6px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06)] mt-auto">
      {pager && <div className="px-6 pt-3 border-b border-slate-200">{pager}</div>}
      <div className="px-6 py-4 flex items-center gap-3">
        {/* Left: End */}
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={onEnd}
          disabled={disabled}
          className="rounded-full border border-slate-400 bg-white text-slate-700 px-5 py-2 text-[14px] font-medium hover:bg-slate-100 disabled:opacity-40 disabled:cursor-not-allowed transition-colors"
        >
          {endLabel ?? "End"}
        </button>

        {/* Menu — opens the question drawer on Details */}
        {onOpenMenu && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={onOpenMenu}
            disabled={disabled}
            className="rounded-full border border-slate-400 bg-white text-slate-700 px-5 py-2 text-[14px] font-medium hover:bg-slate-100 disabled:opacity-40 disabled:cursor-not-allowed transition-colors"
          >
            Menu
          </button>
        )}

        {/* Right cluster */}
        <div className="ml-auto flex items-center gap-3">
          {onAutoRevealChange && (
          <TooltipProvider delayDuration={150}>
            <Tooltip>
              <TooltipTrigger asChild>
                <label
                  className={cn(
                    "flex items-center gap-2 cursor-pointer select-none",
                    disabled && "opacity-40 pointer-events-none",
                  )}
                >
                  <ClipboardCheck
                    className={cn(
                      "w-4 h-4 transition-colors",
                      autoReveal ? "text-[#7f1d1d]" : "text-slate-400",
                    )}
                  />
                  <Switch
                    checked={autoReveal}
                    onCheckedChange={onAutoRevealChange}
                    disabled={disabled}
                    aria-label="Auto-reveal correct answer after answering"
                    className="data-[state=checked]:bg-[#7f1d1d]"
                  />
                </label>
              </TooltipTrigger>
              <TooltipContent side="top" className="max-w-[260px] text-center">
                Toggle on to automatically show correct answers after answering
              </TooltipContent>
            </Tooltip>
          </TooltipProvider>
          )}

          {onPrev && (
            <button
              type="button"
              onClick={onPrev}
              disabled={disabled || prevDisabled}
              className={OUTLINE_PILL_CLASS}
            >
              Previous
            </button>
          )}

          {onAdvance && (
            <button
              type="button"
              onClick={onAdvance}
              disabled={disabled || advanceDisabled}
              className={OUTLINE_PILL_CLASS}
            >
              Next
            </button>
          )}

          {onSkip && !isNext && (
            <button
              type="button"
              onClick={onSkip}
              disabled={disabled || skipDisabled}
              className={OUTLINE_PILL_CLASS}
            >
              Skip
            </button>
          )}

          {primaryVisible && (
            <button
              type="button"
              onClick={isNext ? onNext : onSubmit}
              disabled={disabled || (!isNext && primaryDisabled)}
              className="rounded-full bg-slate-900 text-white px-7 py-2 text-[14px] font-semibold hover:bg-black disabled:opacity-40 disabled:cursor-not-allowed transition-colors"
            >
              {primaryLabel ?? (isNext ? "Next" : "Submit")}
            </button>
          )}
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

import * as React from 'react';
import { cn } from '@/lib/utils';

/**
 * The two-column exam body shared by the LR and RC players.
 *
 * Both players show a reading surface on the left and the question with its
 * answer choices on the right, each column scrolling on its own behind a
 * hairline divider. They drifted apart once already — the LR side lost its page
 * cap and the columns stopped lining up with the bottom bar — so the geometry
 * lives here and nowhere else. A spacing fix is made once.
 *
 * What stays configurable is the column ratio, the page cap, and whether the
 * right column is present at all (RC's Passage Only View, and the LR
 * pool-exhausted state, both render a single full-width pane).
 *
 * Both drill runners currently pass `passage-weighted` with no page cap, so the
 * defaults below are for other callers rather than for them. The doc used to
 * say LR split evenly and capped at max-w-7xl, which stopped being true when
 * the LR pane was rebuilt and was never corrected.
 */

export type DrillSplitRatio = 'even' | 'passage-weighted';

const RATIO_CLASS: Record<DrillSplitRatio, string> = {
  even: 'grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2',
  'passage-weighted': 'grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-[minmax(0,53fr)_minmax(0,47fr)]',
};

interface Props {
  left: React.ReactNode;
  /** Omit for a single full-width column. */
  right?: React.ReactNode;
  ratio?: DrillSplitRatio;
  /** Tailwind cap for the page width, or null to fill the frame. */
  maxWidthClassName?: string | null;
  className?: string;
}

export function DrillSplit({
  left,
  right,
  ratio = 'even',
  maxWidthClassName = 'max-w-7xl',
  className,
}: Props) {
  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        'grid min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-hidden',
        right ? RATIO_CLASS[ratio] : 'grid-cols-1',
        maxWidthClassName && `w-full ${maxWidthClassName} mx-auto`,
        className,
      )}
    >
      {left}
      {right}
    </div>
  );
}

export default DrillSplit;

Tool result:
  // couple of pixels apart. The stimulus carries the extra weight of the serif
  // face; stem and choices stay on the sans stack at the same size.
  //
  // On a short screen 19px pushed choice D and E below the fold, so the size
  // steps down with viewport height (17px floor) and a full question fits
  // without scrolling or zooming. Tall screens still get the 7Sage 19px.
  const baseSize = `calc(${fontScale} * clamp(17px, 2.2vh, 19px))`;
  const officialContentTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    color: "#111827",
    fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif',
    fontSize: baseSize,
    fontWeight: 400,
    lineHeight: 1.5,
  };
  const stimStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    ...officialContentTextStyle,
    fontFamily: "Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif",
    fontSize: baseSize,
    lineHeight: 1.6,
    // Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. Left on `auto` the
    // browser picks the cut from the rendered px size, which drifts as the
    // student changes fontScale. Pin it to the text-optimised end so hairlines
    // stay the same weight at every scale.
    fontOpticalSizing: "none",
    fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
  };
  const stemStyle: React.CSSProperties = officialContentTextStyle;
  // The answer-choice text and letter badges used Tailwind's `text-sm`/`text-xs`,
  // which are hard px values and silently overrode the inherited scaled size —
  // so fontScale grew the stimulus and stem while the answers stayed at 14px.
  const choiceTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = { fontSize: baseSize };

            {aboveChoices && <div className="mt-4">{aboveChoices}</div>}

            <ul className="mt-3 space-y-1.5">
              {answerEntries.map(([key, text]) => {
                const isSelected = selectedAnswer === key;
                const isEliminated = eliminatedAnswers.has(key);
                // Correctness is a fact; colouring is a state. The Choice Note
                // needs the fact (its tag chip goes green for the right letter
                // whether or not Answer is on); the card needs the state.
                const isCorrectFact = correctAnswer === key;
                const showCorrectColour = answerRevealed && isCorrectFact;
                // The choice they committed to, once it is known to be wrong.
                // Without this a wrong answer stayed the same neutral grey as an
                // unrevealed selection, so revealing said what was right without
                // ever saying what they did.
                const isWrongPick = answerRevealed && isSelected && !isCorrectFact;
                // The inline layer follows Explanation, not Answer.
                const inline = inlineNotes ? inlineNotes[key] ?? {} : undefined;
                const noteOpen = !choiceNoteExpanders || openLetters.has(key);
                const showNoteToggle = choiceNoteExpanders && !!inline && hasChoiceNoteProse(inline);
                const revealLocks = answerRevealed && !choicesStayLive;
                const locked = isEliminated || revealLocks || interactionLocked;
                const showVerdict = answerRevealed && (showCorrectColour || isWrongPick);
                // A verdict choice that is also crossed out still needs a way
                // back: Restore moves to the extra column as a real button.
                const showRestoreBeside = showVerdict && isEliminated && choicesStayLive;
                const choiceTextId = `choice-${question.qid}-${key}`;

                return (
                  <li
                    key={key}
                    className={cn(
                      "group relative rounded-lg border transition-all duration-200",
                      "grid grid-cols-[auto_1fr_auto_auto] items-start gap-3 px-3 py-2",
                      showCorrectColour
                        ? "border-green-500 bg-green-50"
                        : isWrongPick
                        ? "border-red-500 bg-red-50"
                        : isSelected
                        ? "border-slate-500 bg-slate-100 shadow-sm"
                        : isEliminated
                        ? "border-transparent bg-white opacity-55 grayscale-[0.5]"
                        : "border-transparent bg-white hover:border-slate-200 hover:bg-slate-50",
                    )}
                  >
                    <button
                      type="button"
                      onClick={() => !locked && onAnswerSelect(key)}
                      disabled={locked}
                      aria-describedby={choiceTextId}
                      className={cn(
                        "mt-0.5 flex h-6 w-6 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded border font-semibold transition-all",
                        showCorrectColour
                          ? "border-green-600 bg-green-500 text-white"
                          : isWrongPick
                          ? "border-red-600 bg-red-500 text-white"
                          : isSelected
                          ? "border-slate-900 bg-slate-900 text-white"
                          : "border-transparent text-slate-950 group-hover:border-slate-300",
                      )}
                      style={choiceBadgeStyle}
                    >
                      {key}
                    </button>
                    {/* The text is not a button: a browser will not start a
                        drag-selection inside one, which is what kept the
                        highlighter off the answer choices. A plain click here
                        still picks the choice; a drag that produced a
                        selection never does. The letter badge stays the
                        keyboard control and describes itself with this text. */}
                    <div
                      id={choiceTextId}
                      ref={(el) => {
                        if (el) choiceRefs.current.set(key, el);
                        else choiceRefs.current.delete(key);
                      }}
                      onClick={(event) => {
                        if (markedRef.current) {
                          markedRef.current = false;
                          return;
                        }
                        if (locked) return;
                        // A click on a highlight is aimed at the highlight
                        // (the eraser has just taken it), not at the answer.
                        const onHighlight = !!(event.target as HTMLElement).closest?.(
                          "[data-highlight-id]",
                        );
                        if (onHighlight && highlightMode === "erase") return;
                        if (highlightMode === "none" || highlightMode === "erase") {
                          onAnswerSelect(key);
                          return;
                        }
                        deferredPick.schedule(() => {
                          // The second press of a double-click marked a word
                          // in the meantime; that was never a pick.
                          if (markedRef.current) return;
                          onAnswerSelect(key);
                        });
                      }}
                      onDoubleClick={() => deferredPick.cancel()}
                      className={cn(
                        "text-left leading-normal transition-colors",
                        !locked && "cursor-pointer",
                        showCorrectColour
                          ? "text-green-900"
                          : isWrongPick
                          ? "text-red-900"
                          : isSelected
                          ? "text-slate-950"

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu-fit\\src\\components\\drill\\official\\ScrollMoreCue.tsx", "content": "import * as React from \"react\";\nimport { ChevronDown } from \"lucide-react\";\n\n/**\n * Whether a scroll container still has content below what is on screen.\n *\n * Rechecks on scroll, when the container resizes, and when anything inside it\n * changes (a new question, an explanation opening, the student's text size).\n */\nexport function useHasMoreBelow(ref: React.RefObject<HTMLElement>): boolean {\n  const [more, setMore] = React.useState(false);\n\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    const el = ref.current;\n    if (!el) return;\n    // A couple of pixels of slack so sub-pixel rounding never leaves the cue\n    // showing at the very bottom.\n    const check = () => setMore(el.scrollTop + el.clientHeight < el.scrollHeight - 2);\n    check();\n    el.addEventListener(\"scroll\", check, { passive: true });\n    const resize = typeof ResizeObserver !== \"undefined\" ? new ResizeObserver(check) : null;\n    resize?.observe(el);\n    const mutation =\n      typeof MutationObserver !== \"undefined\" ? new MutationObserver(check) : null;\n    mutation?.observe(el, { childList: true, subtree: true, characterData: true, attributes: true });\n    return () => {\n      el.removeEventListener(\"scroll\", check);\n      resize?.disconnect();\n      mutation?.disconnect();\n    };\n  }, [ref]);\n\n  return more;\n}\n\n/**\n * The \"more below\" cue for a Question screen column. Most questions fit on a\n * laptop screen, but the longest ones, and any question read at a larger text\n * size, run past the bottom of their column. Without a cue a student can take\n * four choices for five. Render it as the LAST child of the scroll container:\n * it is a zero-height sticky anchor, so it adds no height of its own and the\n * fade sits over the bottom edge of whatever is visible.\n */\nexport function ScrollMoreCue({\n  scrollRef,\n  label = \"More below\",\n}: {\n  scrollRef: React.RefObject<HTMLElement>;\n  label?: string;\n}) {\n  const more = useHasMoreBelow(scrollRef);\n  if (!more) return null;\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"sticky bottom-0 h-0 z-10\" data-testid=\"scroll-more-cue\">\n      <div className=\"pointer-events-none absolute inset-x-0 bottom-0 flex h-14 items-end justify-center bg-gradient-to-t from-white via-white/85 to-transparent pb-2\">\n        <button\n          type=\"button\"\n          onClick={() => {\n            const el = scrollRef.current;\n            if (el) el.scrollBy({ top: el.clientHeight * 0.8, behavior: \"smooth\" });\n          }}\n          className=\"pointer-events-auto inline-flex items-center gap-1 rounded-full border border-slate-300 bg-white px-3 py-1 font-sans text-[13px] font-medium text-slate-700 shadow-sm hover:bg-slate-50\"\n        >\n          {label}\n          <ChevronDown className=\"h-4 w-4\" aria-hidden />\n        </button>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"}

export function ClassroomAssignmentView() {
  const { assignmentId } = useParams<{ assignmentId: string }>();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const assignmentQuery = useStudentAssignment(assignmentId);
  const setQuery = useHomeworkSet(assignmentQuery.assignment?.set_id ?? undefined);
  const assignment = assignmentQuery.assignment;

  const start = () => {
    if (!assignment) return;
    const guidedSetId =
      setQuery.set?.id && setQuery.set.is_guided_set ? setQuery.set.id : undefined;
    const query = launchDrill({
      kind: "assignment",
      assignmentId: assignment.id,
      guidedSetId,
      options: { returnTo: "/classroom" },
    });
    navigate(`/drill?${query}`);
  };

  if (assignmentQuery.loading && !assignment) return <DetailLoading />;
  if (assignmentQuery.error) {

import * as React from 'react';
import { cn } from '@/lib/utils';
import { DrillSplit } from '@/components/drill/DrillSplit';

/**
 * The RC exam body: the shared DrillSplit primitive at the passage-weighted
 * 53/47 ratio, filling its frame rather than taking the LR player's page cap.
 * The column geometry itself lives in DrillSplit so RC and LR cannot drift.
 *
 * Colors come from --rc-bg / --rc-fg rather than Tailwind literals so the
 * accessibility schemes can repaint the reading surface without touching the
 * frame, toolbar or footer around it.
 */

interface Props {
  /** rc-scheme-* class, or empty for the default black-on-white. */
  schemeClassName: string;
  /** Passage Only View: the passage takes the full width and reflows. */
  passageOnly: boolean;
  passage: React.ReactNode;
  question: React.ReactNode;
}

export function RcExamPane({ schemeClassName, passageOnly, passage, question }: Props) {
  return (
    <div className={cn('rc-exam relative flex h-full min-h-0 flex-col', schemeClassName)}>
      <DrillSplit
        ratio="passage-weighted"
        maxWidthClassName={null}
        left={passage}
        right={passageOnly ? undefined : question}
      />
    </div>
  );
}

export interface RcTypeStyles {
  passage: React.CSSProperties;
  question: React.CSSProperties;
  choiceText: React.CSSProperties;
  choiceBadge: React.CSSProperties;
}

/**
 * The LR player's type stack, scaled. Fraunces at 17/1.7 for the reading
 * surface and Helvetica Neue at 16/1.6 for stem and choices; `extraLeading` is
 * added on top of both so the accessibility control cannot flatten the
 * difference between them.
 *
 * Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. Left on `auto` the
 * browser picks the cut from the rendered px size, which drifts as the student
 * changes the scale, so it is pinned to the text-optimised end.
 */
export function rcTypeStyles(fontScale: number, extraLeading: number): RcTypeStyles {
  const base = 16 * fontScale;
  return {
    passage: {
      color: 'var(--rc-fg)',
      fontFamily: 'Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif',
      fontSize: `${17 * fontScale}px`,
      lineHeight: 1.7 + extraLeading,
      fontWeight: 400,
      fontOpticalSizing: 'none',
      fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
    },
    question: {
      color: 'var(--rc-fg)',
      fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif',
      fontSize: `${base}px`,
      lineHeight: 1.6 + extraLeading,
      fontWeight: 400,
    },
    choiceText: { fontSize: `${base}px` },
    choiceBadge: { fontSize: `${base * 0.78}px` },
  };
}

export default RcExamPane;

  }, [question.questionId, question.correctAnswer, revealed, selected, reattemptPick]);

      style={type.question}

      {reattemptMode && (

        {question.choices.map((choice) => {
          /* Blind means blind. `reattemptPick ?? selected` fell back to the
             original answer while the student was being asked to answer again,
             so the pane highlighted the very choice it was hiding — the
             re-attempt was answering itself. */
          const isPicked = reattemptMode
            ? reattemptPick === choice.letter
            : (reattemptPick ?? selected) === choice.letter;
          return (
            <RcChoiceRow
              // Keyed on the question too, so a new question gets fresh rows:
              // nothing a row captured on the last question carries over.
              key={`${question.questionId}:${choice.letter}`}
              choice={choice}
              questionId={question.questionId}
              selected={isPicked}
              eliminated={
                // Hidden outright while they answer again, and never drawn on
                // the choice they have just picked: a row that is selected AND
                // struck through reads as "you crossed out your own answer",
                // when what happened is that they landed on a choice they had
                // eliminated the first time round.
                !reattemptMode && !isPicked && eliminations.includes(choice.letter)
              }
              revealed={showFeedback}
              disabled={showFeedback}
              eliminationDisabled={reattemptMode}
              onSelect={onSelect}
              onToggleElimination={onToggleElimination}

              badgeStyle={type.choiceBadge}
              highlights={highlights}

/* Accessibility color schemes. They repaint the reading surface only: the
   frame, toolbar and footer stay in the official chrome around them. */
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-grey { --rc-bg: #d9d9d9; --rc-fg: #4a4a4a; }
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-purple { --rc-bg: #dcf5dc; --rc-fg: #4b0082; }
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-violet { --rc-bg: #b9a6d4; --rc-fg: #000000; }
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-navy { --rc-bg: #001a4d; --rc-fg: #ffe600; }
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-black { --rc-bg: #000000; --rc-fg: #ffffff; }

.rc-exam-col {
  background: var(--rc-bg);
  color: var(--rc-fg);
}

/* Under a scheme the row fills would fight the chosen palette, so the choice
   rows go transparent and their state reads from the border instead. */
.rc-exam[class*='rc-scheme-'] .rc-choice { background-color: transparent; }
.rc-exam[class*='rc-scheme-'] .rc-choice-text { color: inherit; }

/* Slate-on-black is unreadable, so the darkest schemes invert the badge. */
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-navy .rc-choice-badge-selected,
.rc-exam.rc-scheme-black .rc-choice-badge-selected {
  background: var(--rc-fg);
  border-color: var(--rc-fg);
  color: var(--rc-bg);
}

Previous/Next sat entirely below the fold (737-809px), so every question needed a page scroll just to move on. The controls around the question took ~295px.\n\n## Change\n- `OfficialDrillFrame` fills the viewport by default (`fillViewport` now defaults to true); both columns scroll inside the card.\n- Welcome + Tutor fold into the breadcrumb row (one header row instead of two).\n- The question-number ovals move into the bottom bar's single row (`OfficialSmartDrillBar`); tighter padding on the toolbar, navigator and frame. Every button stays and keeps its behaviour.\n- LR columns split 42/58 (`question-weighted` in `DrillSplit`); RC keeps 53/47.\n- Tighter gaps between answer choices (LR and RC). No change to text size or to the student's text-size setting.\n- New `ScrollMoreCue`: a \"More below\" pill + fade on any column with content out of sight (LR both columns, RC question column; RC fade follows the accessibility scheme colour).\n\nControls now take ~160px; the question gets 540px at 1536x695.\n\n## Measured: all 3,965 LR questions rendered in the real pane\n| Window | Fit with nothing hidden |\n|---|---|\n| 1920x950 | 3,903 (98%) |\n| 1440x790 | 3,767 (95%) |\n| 1536x695 (reference) | 3,558 (90%) |\n| 1366x625 | 2,149 (54%) |\n| 1280x600 | 873 (22%) |\n\nBefore: 0 at 1536x695 with Next on screen. The remainder scroll inside their column with the cue. Whether to also lower the text floor on short screens is an open decision for Joshua.\n\n## Verification\n- In-app browser on :8094 (tester account): LR single question, 25-question set (all ovals inline), Smart Drill with Notes panel docked, RC passage 1188, 1000x700 stacked layout, cue appears/disappears correctly.\n- `npm run typecheck` clean. Drill + RC tests: 30 files, 214 passed.\n- Full suite: 13 failures, all in untouched areas (booking, switchyard, Mainhub, activity centre). The same files fail on untouched `main` (1746b0d); the one difference is the flaky 7s `AssistantComposer` test.\n- Not viewed: Blind Review and the section review walk-through (need completed-section data), a timed full section's navigator (would start a timed section). ## assistant

The Question screen fix is built and waiting for your approval. It's on PR [vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db#136](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/136) and is **not merged or live**. Students won't see any of it until you approve and I publish it.

**Before and after screenshots, which you asked for, aren't done.** I can only save screenshots to a file from a browser that isn't signed in, and signing it in would mean handling the tester's password. Instead, I left the in-app browser pane open on the new version. It has four questions: three short ones and PT114-S2-Q23, the longest in the bank.

## What changed

- **Next and Previous are always on screen.** Before, they sat below the fold on every question. Now the page never scrolls, and a long question scrolls inside its own column.
- **The controls went from about 295px to about 160px.** The welcome text moved into the title row, and the question-number ovals moved into the bottom bar. Every button is still there and does the same thing.
- **The choices column is wider**, with 42% for the stimulus and 58% for the choices. No stimulus overflowed at 1536×695 or at any larger size I measured.
- **The gaps between choices are tighter** on both LR and RC. Text size is unchanged, and so is each student's own text-size setting.
- **A "More below" button appears whenever something is out of sight** and disappears once the student reaches the bottom. On RC, the fade follows whichever colour scheme the student picked.

## How many of the 3,965 LR questions fit with nothing hidden

| Window | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 1920×950 | 0 | 3,903 (98%) |
| 1440×790 | 0 | 3,767 (95%) |
| 1536×695 (your laptop) | 0 | 3,558 (90%) |
| 1366×625 | 0 | 2,149 (54%) |
| 1280×600 | 0 | 873 (22%) |

"Before" is zero everywhere because Next was always below the fold. The rest scroll inside their column, with the "More below" button showing.

## Your call: smaller laptops

On short screens, text can already shrink with the window's height, but never below 17px. If that floor were 15px or 16px:

| Window | 17px (now) | 16px | 15px |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1536×695 | 3,558 | 3,690 | 3,793 |
| 1366×625 | 2,149 | 3,135 | 3,454 |
| 1280×600 | 873 | 1,917 | 2,879 |

This isn't the per-question sizing you were worried about. It's the same mechanism that's already live: the size depends only on the window height, so every question on a given laptop has the same size, and nothing flickers or changes from one question to the next. A student's own text setting still multiplies on top of it. The tradeoff is simply smaller text on small laptops.

I'd go to 16px. It takes 1366×625 from 54% to 79%, and the text stays close to today's size. Do you want 16px, 15px, or to keep 17px?

## Checks

- **Viewed on the tester account:**
  - a single question and a 25-question set, where all the ovals fit in the bar
  - Smart Drill with the Notes panel open
  - an RC passage (it was hiding choice E; that's fixed now)
  - a narrow 1000px window
- **Tests:**
  - Typecheck is clean, and all drill and RC tests pass (214).
  - The full suite has 13 failures in areas I didn't touch. They fail on untouched main too.
- **Not viewed:**
  - Blind Review and the section review walk-through, which need a completed section on the account.
  - A timed full section, which would have started a timed section. All three use the same frame, but I haven't seen them rendered.
- **The fifth screen, Recall's section stage,** turned out not to use the shared frame. It already fits the screen on its own, so I left it alone.

## Other things you should know

- Opening screens on the tester account started a Smart Drill session and an RC session (passage 1188). I didn't answer anything.
- `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` in the old worktree is still gutted and needs the `git checkout --` restore I gave you last time. I kept it out of the commit.
- The plan file is updated with all of this.

No. On a laptop or desktop screen it stays side by side, just as it was. You're seeing it stacked because the browser pane is narrow.

The pane in your screenshot is only 864px wide. When the window is narrower than 1024px, the question screen stacks the passage above the question. That rule was already on main before I touched anything: `grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-[…]` in `DrillSplit.tsx`, and live lsatprep.study stacks the same way at that width. I didn't add it or change it.

To confirm, I switched the pane to your laptop size, 1536×695, and it's side by side: stimulus on the left, question and all five choices on the right, Next at the bottom. At that size the columns measure 587px and 811px.

The only change to the columns is that the choices side is a bit wider than before (58% instead of 47%), which is what we agreed in Q9.1. Nothing moved to vertical.

The app resets the pane's size when my turn ends, so it may go back to narrow and stack again. To see it side by side yourself, drag the pane wider (past about 1024px), or pick a larger size from the pane's Viewport menu.

The one open question from before still stands: 16px, 15px, or keep 17px for small laptops.

## assistant

I set the lowest text size on short screens to 16px, down from 17px. It's on PR [vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db#136](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/136), still not merged or live.

I measured all 3,965 LR questions again, this time rendered at the real new size:

| Window | Text size | Fit with nothing hidden (was) |
|---|---|---|
| 1920×950 | 19px | 3,903 (98%), unchanged |
| 1440×790 | 17.4px | 3,767 (95%), unchanged |
| 1536×695 (your laptop) | 16px | **3,690 (93%)** (was 3,558) |
| 1366×625 | 16px | **3,136 (79%)** (was 2,149) |
| 1280×600 | 16px | **1,927 (49%)** (was 873) |

Why 16px and not 15px:
- **16px is 1px smaller than today** on short screens and doesn't change taller screens at all.
- **15px would fit more** on the smallest laptops, but it starts to look small next to LawHub, and small print adds strain in a timed section.
- **Every question on a given screen shows at the same size.** That's what you were worried about with per-question sizing, and it doesn't happen here.
- **A student's own text-size setting still works on top of this.**
- **Questions that still don't fit** show the "More below" button.

The drill and RC tests all pass, and typecheck is clean.

The browser pane is set to your laptop size, 1536×695, so you can see the result now. It may go back to narrow when my turn ends. If it does, drag the pane wider than about 1024px to see the two columns side by side again.

When you're happy with how it looks, say so and I'll merge and publish it.

I'm confused; when I push to main, it should automatically go to lovable, and I can publish it. Why isn't it doing that?

## assistant

It is working that way. The push reached Lovable. The part that caught us out was timing.

**What happened with LSAT U:**
1. Merging the PR put the code on main on GitHub.
2. Lovable picked it up automatically. Its edit history shows the merge commit `28e46d7` as completed. Pushing to main builds a new preview in Lovable, but lsatprep.study doesn't change until you hit Publish (or I run `deploy_project`, which is the same button).
3. I ran publish right after the merge, before Lovable had finished pulling it in. That publish shipped the old version.
4. I ran publish a second time a few minutes later, and that one carried the new code. It's live now.

So your flow is right: push to main, Lovable picks it up by itself, then you publish. The only catch is to wait until the change shows up in Lovable before you hit Publish.

**The second push was a different repo.** When you said "push to main", the LSAT U code was already on main. So I pushed the vault (`ai-os`), which holds your notes and logs. That repo isn't connected to Lovable and never shows up there, which is expected.

To publish LSAT U yourself next time:
1. Open the project in Lovable.
2. Check that the latest commit appears in its history.
3. Hit Publish.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

# Session Handoff — LSAT U: Question screen fits one laptop screen (shipped)

## Where it started
Joshua pasted the previous handoff. The Drill page and Home were already live, and the Question screen was next. He cleared the two OPEN items from that handoff, then worked through grilling Round 9 on the Question screen. He was worried about breaking things, so the approach was: no change to student data, a branch plus a PR, and nothing published without his approval. He approved, and it was merged and published.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **Q9.1:** The goal is that the whole question and all five choices are in view with no scrolling. Scrolling is only a fallback. To get there: shrink the chrome, make the choices column wider, and tighten the gaps between choices. The student's own text-size setting is never overridden, and a student who turns text up gets a scrolling column.
- **Q9.2:** Previous and Next are always on screen. The question-number ovals sit inside the bottom bar. Welcome and Tutor fold into the title row. The toolbar and gap padding are trimmed.
- **Q9.3:** Applies to every screen that uses `OfficialDrillFrame`. Recall's section stage turned out not to use it; it already fits through its own CSS and was not changed.
- **Text floor:** Joshua said "do what you think is best", so the short-screen floor went from 17px to 16px. It is still based on viewport height and is multiplied by the student's own setting.
- **Shipped:** PR vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db#136, commits `887bdb3` and `bee289c`, merged as `28e46d7`. Published with Lovable `deploy_project` (free), project `53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`. The first deploy went out before Lovable had synced the merge and published the old build. The second deploy worked.
